# Water gauntlet execution

Verified: 2026-10-21 against the in-place startup-capacity repair, actual production GPU stepping, source-volume progression and rendered source/pool. Full seven-fall acceptance remains open.

## Progress discipline

1. A failed gate is evidence, not product progress by itself. Before the next progress checkpoint, execute a concrete repair or bounded causal diagnosis of its measured defect, unless a verified external wait or approval boundary prevents it. An unexecuted plan, tracker refresh or repeated status report does not satisfy this rule.
2. A runtime rejection stops promotion. When safe and useful, a separately labeled bounded offline diagnosis may continue far enough to test the required physical behavior; preserve the failed runtime gate. Do not repeatedly stop before impact and then imply that water behavior was tested.
3. An unavailable rendered view blocks visual acceptance, not independent source diagnosis, implementation or compute execution. Re-poll the same live handle; do not restart after a timeout. Mark the whole goal blocked only when the required consecutive-turn audit passes and no meaningful authorized work remains.
4. Every current action names the actual implementation, input/state, measured failure, next executable step and evidence that would prove improvement. Distinguish planned, built, running, terminal and verified. Check process/worker state before claiming activity. Current result and current next action govern; prior checkpoints are historical.
5. Consultations must be self-contained: supplied code/version, geometry, units, observed facts, measured limits and unknowns. Pro and Gemini cannot inspect local code or visuals. Use their answer for a concrete implementation decision and local test; avoid another generic recommendation or an endless code-audit ladder.
6. Make successful intermediate changes inspectable in the live or clearly labeled candidate scene, and judge motion over time. Preserve all original requirements and acceptance gates. Administrative work supports the water goal and does not replace it.

Nonhydrostatic response screen: 27 CPU controls pass for an independently discretized, constant-depth, flat-bed 1D projection based on https://arxiv.org/html/1506.03316. Three depths, three resolutions, rest/Fourier/descending-Gaussian inputs verify the constraint, analytic Fourier response and orthogonal-projection energy identity (maximum defect 7.46e-14 J). At 512 cells, the 4 mm film retains only 0.031211 percent of the imposed Gaussian vertical kinetic energy after projection; 25 mm retains 1.178 percent and 100 mm retains 13.232 percent. This is a fixed-depth response screen, NOT a mass-admitting impact, transport, wet/dry or lip-release simulation and NOT the paper's full numerical scheme. The accounted projection loss is not validated physical impact dissipation. Do not install a naive vertical-impulse projection as a qualified impact closure or generalize this result to reject all nonhydrostatic methods. Coupled source audit: the guarded impact draft uses core prepare_advection_upwind, whose separate staggered-density transport does not consume the verified VOF UFl/UFg. ## Goal and authority

Build realistic, interactive water whose behavior produces its appearance: approach, flow-dependent crest release, high free fall, impact in a raised plunge pool, storage, a smaller spill waterfall, sloped runoff, branching rapids and the river. Flood changes travel with arriving water. Preserve the existing pleasing river optics, source accounting, sleeping dry ground, object controls and camera inspection. No baked animation or fixed water motion. Use Yosemite waterfalls and Merced River rapids as the realism reference.

Terrain beauty is secondary while water remains unresolved. Return to cliff, banks, rocks and vegetation after the water behavior, appearance and runtime gate passes. Complete the final combined scene review afterward.

The parent may change algorithms, experiment order, worker scopes and the research workflow within this goal. A smaller pilot is a milestone, not a smaller definition of success. Only the user may reduce the goal or accept a material tradeoff in realism.

## One home for each record

| Record | Home |
| --- | --- |
| Planned work and completion | `public/planmap/topics.json`, topic `ground3d-water-surface` |
| How the water works and explored approaches | `docs/architecture/domains/world3d-river-water.md` |
| This repeatable research and execution loop | This file |
| Human-facing map | `.project-map/index.html`, generated from the records above |
| Style preference | `.project-map/preferences.json` |
| Active ownership and handoff | Agora task `agora-e707` |
| Raw experiment logs, captures and frozen operators | `.agent/scratch/waterfall-gauntlet/` |

The existing `Water gauntlet 01` through `08` features summarize milestones. The `Water task Wxx | Mx | ...` features are the atomic checkbox list. Count only those tasks, once. The map embeds an offline snapshot of that list; it is not a separate status database. Keep the prior gauntlet page as the visual experiment archive.

Status rules: `done` requires the named evidence; `active` means an unfinished task with work or research selected; `specced` means queued; `parked` names an explicit dependency; `superseded` preserves an abandoned method with its reason. Show technical failures as Research needed. Show unavailable access, services or necessary decisions as Waiting with an exact dependency. Distinguish implementation present, focused checks passing, rendered acceptance and owner acceptance.

## Iterative loop

1. Read the current objective, canonical checklist, explored-method record and last Agora checkpoint. Check the actual goal and worker state. Do not restart previous experiments just because a session is new.
2. Select one bounded slice on the path to the next milestone. Write its hypothesis, expected observable result, owner, allowed files, dependencies, runtime/accuracy budgets, checks, and stop condition. Refresh the map before a long run.
3. Use Agora identity, exact session provenance and narrow locks. Give implementation work to GPT-6.1-sol at medium effort as requested. The map agent only draws the map. The parent owns scope, synthesis and acceptance; use a separate reviewer for consequential physics and visual gates where available.
4. Implement and run the smallest experiment that can change the decision. Prefer a complete moving water sequence. Use existing reference checks; keep expensive audits out of the production loop. Name the next visible deliverable. As soon as a valid advancing state exists, expose it in the isolated interactive viewer with its source identity, physical time and remaining failures. Do not wait for final acceptance to show working progress, and never invent motion to make an incomplete solver look live.
5. Independently review behavior, rendered motion and total cost. When a process-control or execution-context change affects a deadline, first verify termination on the same disposable wrapper/child shape. Include failed stop attempts and cleanup overruns in actual total cost. Separate cold compilation, setup and warm simulation measurements; never remove their cost from the result by silently warming outside a coordinated window. Save a reproducible command or URL, source identity, physical duration, first impact/overflow times, conservation/error measurements, timing breakdown and front/side/moving-camera captures.
6. On success, check off only the proved tasks, record evidence and limits, refresh the map, then take its next eligible task. A numerical pass alone cannot close a visual task.
7. On a technical failure, preserve the accepted state and capture one bounded reproduction. Enter Research needed immediately. Record what was expected, what happened, the first failed operation, what has already been tried, and the discriminating question. Keep compact deterministic first-invalid-row and predicate metadata in the ordinary guard path, and capture relevant operands on failure. This avoids spending another shared GPU window only to locate an unspecified flag. Full phase tracing remains a bounded diagnostic, not work repeated every physical interval.
8. Consult the existing ChatGPT Pro conversation through the in-app browser. Send the reproduction, measured timings, current representation, constraints and prior attempts. Ask Pro to challenge the architecture, use relevant recent primary research, and propose a bounded experiment with stop/go criteria. Do not ask it to lower the user's goal.
9. Give Pro ample time. Read its completed answer before calling the consultation complete. Check about every five minutes during longer research; request progress updates only if the interface supports them, without cancelling or repeatedly resubmitting. Keep useful independent work moving while waiting.
10. Treat Pro's answer as research input. Verify material source claims and distinguish published findings from its proposed adaptation. Record accepted/rejected advice and why. Revise the slice, then return to step 3. Do not rerun an unchanged failure indefinitely.

After two bounded repair/optimization attempts without a meaningful improvement, seek an architecture review from Pro before extending the same approach. This is a method review, not a terminal project blocker. If Pro or another external dependency is unavailable, record the exact wait and continue other authorized work. Do not promise that external dependencies can never block execution; do not mark the objective complete, paused or blocked merely because implementation is difficult.

Every milestone and change of direction refreshes the map. Before answering progress questions, reconcile the map with the live task/worker state. At session end record what changed, what failed, the next action and the required proof; release locks and retire Agora presence. Leave the project unfinished when acceptance remains open.

## Earlier selected slice: low-order geometric cascade (now frozen)

The earlier Pro recommendation was to freeze the high-order geometric prototype as a reference and build a separate lower-order conservative MAC/VOF runtime. This is the selected candidate to test, not a claim that a paper or Pro has validated it. Preserve all earlier code and evidence.

Owner: parent orchestrator; implementation worker: GPT-6.1-sol, medium. Allowed implementation area for the next dispatch: a separately named experimental water module and ignored runner/proof files, selected and locked before work. Native river material, waterfall defaults and live scene remain protected until the candidate passes review. This workflow setup does not itself launch that implementation worker.

Deliverable: ten physical seconds computed live through inlet -> crest -> roughly 2 m fall -> impact -> raised basin -> secondary overflow -> branching outlet. Use simple solids and an initially manageable domain; Pro proposes 8 x 6 x 3 m at 0.125 m spacing. The 2 m pilot must lead directly to the full-height scene after acceptance.

Reproducible source input: 0.03 m3/s from 0-1 s, 0.12 from 1-4 s, 0.30 from 4-6 s, then zero through 10 s. Total released volume is 0.99 m3. Also provide a live source control. Source depth, area, discharge and incoming momentum must be compatible. Water motion and spill onset are solved, not timed animations or a second emitter.

Use one compatible low-order velocity/pressure/transport representation, shared positive volume/momentum donors, actual occupied geometry, local guided reconstruction and explicit treatment of weak support. Preserve positive material, physical momentum, wet connectivity and wall reaction. Reduce unreliable independent velocity modes through a justified conservative representation if needed; do not delete the water, invent support mass, mix across air or hide a failed residual. A real disconnected drop and an attached thin film need different treatment.

Reuse the existing direct momentum, hydrostatic/contact and no-cross-air reference checks. Permit at most one frozen tiny-remnant experiment before the complete cascade. Do not start a general certification framework. Thin films, wetting/seepage, aerodynamic breakup and entrained air remain explicit later obligations; a single-phase coarse pilot cannot claim to resolve them.

Render current liquid geometry as continuous water using the existing optical infrastructure where valid. Show low/high flow and shutoff from front, side and moving cameras. Do not mask holes with opaque white water, excessive smoothing or foam.

## Acceptance and cost

Set numerical and visual accuracy budgets before looking at candidate results. Keep physical mass ownership, source/outlet exchange and direct external-impulse accounting strict. Measure interface error, reconstruction first-moment error, energy loss and resolved behavior under fixed-time spatial/timestep refinement. A new discretization need not reproduce the old near-singular coefficient vector, but it must preserve and demonstrate the required physics.

Report wall time / simulated physical time, including every substep, transport, reconstruction, pressure, transfers and render/readback cost. Report CPU and GPU separately; if GPU timing is unavailable, say so. Record hardware and first-frame versus steady behavior. Pro's provisional water budget is 8 ms per 16.7 ms frame on the actual target adapter; it is a candidate engineering budget to measure and review, not an achieved result. Do not improve reported frame rate by silently slowing physical time.

After the first complete run, allow two optimizations chosen from measured dominant costs. Stop the candidate on failed physical invariants, exhausted representation capacity or inability to meet the declared runtime gate. Save the failure and return to the Pro research loop. Do not return to indefinite QR/KKT rescue.

Before native acceptance: full-height launch/fall/impact, pool backpressure and secondary spill, low-flow runoff, source-off, delayed floods, overflow/recession, branch behavior, foam and floating objects, underwater/moving-camera optics, native river preservation, startup and steady runtime. Then complete terrain polish and repeat the combined-scene checks.

## W05 contract: cascade-runtime-v1

Declared before implementation on 2026-10-07. These are engineering acceptance limits for a candidate, not achieved results. The parent owns this contract. The implementation worker is `/root/cascade_runtime_v1`, GPT-6.1-sol at medium effort. An independent read-only review checked fixture storage, subcell sheet thickness and failure risks before dispatch.

Target host: AMD Ryzen 7 3700X, 16 logical processors, 31.93 GiB RAM, Windows x64, Node 22.19.0, verified through the local runtime. The first CPU implementation uses Float64. GPU adapter and timestamp support are not yet measured; any GPU implementation must identify them and requalify its actual stored precision before claiming acceptance. CPU proof alone cannot satisfy the complete rendered runtime gate.

Allowed new source paths: `src/systems/world3d/river/cascadeRuntimeV1.ts`, directly named `cascadeRuntimeV1*.ts` helpers, and `src/systems/world3d/river/__tests__/cascadeRuntimeV1.test.ts`. This separates the first visual feedback from solver implementation without overlapping file ownership.

### Fixture and observable result

Use meters and `(x,y,z)` with `y` up. Domain: 8 x 3 x 6 m, baseline cell size 0.125 m. The crest is at `x=1.5, y=2.5`. Use a short approach, source section 0.5 m wide and 0.125 m deep. The specified discharge pulse gives incoming speeds 0.48, 1.92 and 4.8 m/s. Start dry; count any explicitly introduced initial material separately. The pulse is exactly 0.99 m3 and stops at 6 s; split timesteps at source events.

The receiving basin runs from `x=1.5` to `5.5`, is 0.75 m wide, has floor `y=0.5` and spill lip `y=0.625`. Its nominal storage before overflow is 0.375 m3. Keep other trapped storage below 0.05 m3. Provide a broad spill opening, a smaller drop, sloped runoff and two unobstructed descending branches to the downstream boundary. The high-flow gravity-only landing estimate is near `x=4.57`, inside the basin. This estimate checks fixture geometry; it must not prescribe the solved trajectory.

The useful full-run gate is impact before 2 s, secondary overflow before 7 s, water arriving at both branch exits before 9 s, and at least 0.15 m3 total outlet exchange by 10 s with at least 0.03 m3 through each branch. These times test whether the fixture exercises the objective. A miss requires diagnosis; do not add timed emitters, alter the pulse, or silently reshape the fixture after inspecting results.

Low-flow falling water is roughly 1 cm thick at this width and fall speed, below the 0.125 m grid. The first candidate therefore needs two-interface/slab support for actual thin sheets, including their volume and connectivity. A single-plane volume fraction is insufficient by itself. Geometry capacity failure must be explicit; fattening the water or discarding dilute cells is not a valid substitute. Local wet support, not global connected-component membership, governs permitted aggregation.

### Numerical and visual gates

| Measurement | Gate |
| --- | --- |
| Actual occupied volume plus measured boundary exchange | Maximum mass-volume closure error at most 1e-9 m3 over the Float64 run |
| Direct material momentum, all three axes | Cumulative residual at most 1e-6 kg m/s per axis; only gravity, measured wall reaction and boundary exchange are external impulses |
| Hydrostatic rest | Speed below 1e-7 m/s and analytic pressure-head checks |
| Represented incompressibility | Maximum divergence at most 1e-7 /s on actual wet/cut geometry |
| Material ownership | No positive-volume deletion, overlap, cross-air donor mixing or negative allocations; a numerical tolerance is not a clipping policy |
| Source-off | No source volume after 6 s; material already released keeps traveling |
| Reconstruction displacement | Volume-weighted mean centroid displacement at most 0.05h per reconstruction; retain cumulative signed and absolute defects |
| Numerical restriction/remap energy loss | At most 5% of available mechanical energy; distinguish physical impact dissipation and potential-energy changes |
| Closed render mesh | Volume within 2% of represented liquid; account for any open boundaries separately |

Use independent actual occupancy and current-velocity sums, not a shadow ledger that defines its own closure. Reuse the three-axis isolated momentum/co-translation checks in `waterfallLiquidBehavior.test.ts`, connected-U geometry in `behavior-vof-worker/U-local-frozen.json`, and hydrostatic/contact/source-history/exit-consumption contracts from the existing crest controls. At most one frozen remnant-aggregation experiment precedes the cascade. A failed prerequisite freezes the candidate and its exact state for diagnosis.

After the first valid full run, compare baseline `h=.125`, a halved timestep at that spacing, and `h=.0625` with compatible smaller steps. Sample at physical times 1, 4, 6, 8 and 10 s and event crossings. Agreement limits: impact time 0.05 s, overflow time 0.15 s, basin level 0.25h, total exchanged volume 5%, each branch cumulative exchange 10% of its refined value, whole-state center of mass 0.25h. Agreement is not a claimed formal convergence order. Record physical losses and numerical dissipation separately; do not enlarge limits to pass a failed result without new evidence and a revised contract.

Front, side and moving-camera live views must show source changes, crest release, continuous falling water, impact, rising basin and pool-driven spill. Report absent/unresolved effects. No frame replay, visible particle beads, fixed curtains or foam hiding missing geometry. The outside-camera surface view may be reused only within its documented limits; density-based meshing that omits dilute water cannot establish geometry acceptance. Native river optics stay protected until integration.

### Runtime, stop condition and workflow improvement

Measure ten physical seconds without pacing. More than ten seconds of end-to-end water work fails the real-time gate. Product allocation is at most 4.8 seconds of total water work per ten physical seconds, and p95 at most 8 ms per 16.7 ms presentation interval. Count all substeps, transport, pressure, reconstruction, transfer, mesh upload and water rendering. Record startup separately, active/wet cells, pressure iterations, CPU stage timings, available GPU timings and wall-clock lag. Include costs even if a worker thread hides their main-thread impact.

Run the necessary physical controls first, then attempt the complete sequence immediately. Preserve the first physical failure or representation-capacity failure and report it; do not continue accumulating invalid water. Permit two repairs/optimizations chosen from actual evidence, then consult Pro before extending the same approach. A physics pass without rendered motion and complete timing does not close W09-W11. A small pilot remains preparation for full-height water, not the final goal.

Workflow improvement for this run: front-load the complete hydraulic sequence and record one compact machine-readable report with event times, physical defects and stage costs. Avoid another chain of increasingly expensive isolated algebra checks that never reaches impact. At every experiment and milestone ask, "How can I improve this flow?" Record the specific improvement, its evidence, and any change to the next action. Keep the objective and acceptance bar fixed while changing execution methods. For ignored scratch viewers, identify the served client and worker build in the UI and verify their match before accepting browser evidence. A fresh file on disk may still be served through an old development-server transform. Before batched saves or captures of ignored scratch builds, use new build paths for every routed scratch dependency. ## W06 revised experiment: shared transport and arrival contact

Selected on 2026-10-08 after completed Pro research and separate construction/feasibility reviews. Hypothesis: one conforming transport partition, common corrected faces and synchronized positive donors can remove the first-source overlap while permitting finite impact and bounded thin reconstruction. This is an experimental adaptation of modified EMFPA, not a published guarantee for our cut carrier. The W05 fixture, pulse, numerical limits and full objective remain unchanged.

Owner: parent; implementation: existing GPT-6.1-sol medium worker. Allowed files are the existing `cascadeRuntimeV1.ts`, `cascadeRuntimeV1Geometry.ts`, `cascadeRuntimeV1Projection.ts`, directly named `cascadeRuntimeV1*.ts` helpers and the focused test file. New proof goes in `.agent/scratch/waterfall-gauntlet/cascade-shared-remap/`. Archive the current sources with hashes there before editing. Do not change native water, old failure captures, package/config files, canonical records or Git state. No new dependency or general geometry library is required by this experiment. Report any necessary scope change before writing outside these paths.

Construction contract:

1. Build only the wet neighborhood and empty regions reachable during the step. Give stationary source/wall planes and shared faces/edges/vertices canonical identities. Preserve actual finite rims. Do not infer incidence from proximity or snap arbitrary near-wall water. Fixed-axis constrained intersections use their canonical coordinate. Reliable orientation predicates must distinguish representational rounding from positive occupied overlap.
2. Build a conforming wet/empty partition with common face refinement. Share each geometric vertex prediction across every incident partition face. A connected wet sector may provide a documented kinematic average; this does not replace physical normal-trace continuity. Reject unresolved multi-sector vertices instead of averaging across air or making separately moving copies that leave cracks. Empty extension preserves wet normal moments and wall zero-normal values as hard data. Assign remaining empty-face moments once, without requiring empty divergence to vanish or introducing water mass there.
3. Use one corrected backface per shared face. Match its swept volume to the actual carrier face integral by a face-interior correction, preserving shared corners/edges. Split sign-changing flux patches. Reject a degenerate correction denominator, excessive cap displacement, folding, self-intersection or invalid receiver capacity. Source and outlet faces must participate in this ownership construction. Record the cap-displacement bound before the run.
4. For this bounded variant, permit a positive tetrahedral fan only after verifying a closed oriented boundary, a valid star kernel, shared triangulation and complete non-overlapping coverage. A non-star-shaped receiver is a saved method failure. Never feed a nonconvex surface to the existing convex moment routine, take absolute signed volumes, or call signed cancellation positive allocation. Volume and momentum use the same positive intersections with the old physical wet geometry.
5. Reconstruct jointly inside disjoint receiver regions, then compress onto the fixed grid using transported interface guides. Preserve paired sheet interfaces and separated wet components; source labels do not define connectivity. Reject unresolved topology instead of fattening or deleting water. Retire obsolete partition planes. Fit the physical velocity to donated momentum using the actual reconstructed centroid; measure reconstruction displacement, restriction/remap energy and capacity, with no shadow moment ledger or center-of-mass correction. Before simulations, record the chosen slope transfer and any energy limiter; conserving the mean alone does not qualify their behavior.
6. Retain the actual-cut kinetic metric provisionally. Replace same-cell equality of every velocity coefficient with the physical shared normal trace: one independent condition on an axis-aligned face and sufficient affine normal conditions on oblique triangles. Tangential jumps and locally separated U arms must remain possible. A retained pressure implementation still needs the missing analytic head and connected-U checks before W08 can close.
7. Classify the opening at `x=1.25`, `y=[2.5,2.625]`, `z=[2.75,3.25]` as an upstream computational boundary with a virtual reservoir. Admit `Q dt` and its momentum exactly once through donors; do not inject an overlapping resident slab. Keep advected source momentum separate from any pressure-boundary impulse and derive the latter from actual constraint multipliers. Use a documented first-order gravity/source stage; never give newly admitted material a full prior resident step. Split at the existing source events.
8. Detect finite solid/liquid arrival using the same transport stage, bracket the event, construct actual contact, apply measured wall or internal impulse and advance the remainder. Departure releases the finite rim. Persistent vanishing steps or capacity failures near contact are failures, not a solution. Single-phase immediate coalescence remains an explicit approximation; unresolved air-film physics is not claimed.

Verification sequence: first run the frozen two-step source cases at `.005` and `1/240` seconds, the shared-boundary counterexample, and translating oblique slab. Then reuse the inlet-free `.03` second basin-contact case and its empty-solids capacity comparison. Repair the limited tests' missing absolute-volume and translation checks. Use the existing physical controls, with explicit analytic head and connected-U checks, before attempting the unchanged ten-second cascade. Save the first source donor allocation for one independent integration outside the hot loop. Each test has a concrete failure mode; do not add a broad static certification project.

Before each simulation, record source identity, configuration, rejection/substep bounds and wall-clock cap. Freeze at the first unresolved physical, topology or capacity failure with the accepted and attempted states. Permit at most two evidence-driven local repairs; a missing shared construction returns to research. A test cannot pass by widening an overlap epsilon, using unmeasured zero diagnostics, omitting work from timings or silently slowing simulation time. Once controls pass, run the complete cascade immediately; then update the live viewer with a fresh served-build identity and review front, side and moving views. W06-W11 remain unfinished until their actual evidence exists.

Workflow improvement: make the geometry validity conditions executable at their point of use, preserve the first rejected state, and inspect that saved state instead of repeatedly rerunning to add logs. Keep heavy independent audits outside the production step while counting all physical transport, contact, reconstruction and pressure work. The map must show the actual worker state and next failed/passed gate; research review alone closes no checkbox.

Reconstruction consumes that record. Consistent forward interface trajectories across fixed subregions are a missing operation; old-coordinate donor fragments and corrected backward caps are not endpoint positions. For transverse crossing, follow the physical entering/leaving sector with the same predictor and elapsed time. At zero common normal speed with unequal tangential traces, existing wet-sector/facet ownership must determine the required motion; otherwise freeze the ambiguous physical boundary or edge/corner. Do not average across air or unrelated sectors. A full-affine geometry oracle is explicitly separate from the pressure runtime's current six diagonal-linear velocity DOFs. ## W06 GPU particle candidate: declared contract

Declared 2026-10-08 before implementation. Name: cascadeParticleV1. Parent owns acceptance; GPT-6.1-sol medium owns implementation. Allowed new files: src/systems/world3d/river/cascadeParticleV1*.ts and directly named focused tests under its __tests__ folder. Disposable viewer, manifests, scripts and captured states belong in .agent/scratch/waterfall-gauntlet/cascade-particle-v1/. Read-only reuse of cascadeFixture/cascadePulse and existing renderer-owned GPU scaffolds is allowed. Keep native water materials, scenes, source controls, previous candidates, package/config files and canonical records unchanged. Acquire exact Agora locks before edits. Parent runs one scoped dependency-header pass after the wave.

Use the W05 fixture, opening, pulse and event gates unchanged: domain 8 x 3 x 6 m, source opening at x=1.25, y=[2.5,2.625], z=[2.75,3.25], .99 m3 over six seconds, two-meter fall, .375 m3 nominal basin capacity, pool-driven spill and two outlets. Impact before 2 s, spill before 7 s, both exits before 9 s, total outlet at least .15 m3 and each at least .03 m3 by 10 s remain the useful-sequence checks. No timed downstream emitter. The pilot is preparation for full-height native water, not final acceptance.

Run one unchanged executable at particle spacing d=.02 m as a coarse engineering check, then d=.01 m for refinement. Only if these justify it, run a predeclared short d=.005 m first-fall comparison. Coarse water does not establish thin-sheet acceptance: the low-flow falling thickness is about .0096 m. No adaptive splitting, new pressure framework, simultaneous FLIP candidate, hybrid shallow-water coupling or renderer-created thickness in this assignment.

### Fixed method and implementation manifest

Use published DFSPH with the cubic spline kernel and support radius R=2d (four particle radii); rest density 1000 kg/m3 and material quantum d^3 m3. Pin SPlisHSPlasH commit f3f677140761db7637b5443beb54f19f1f835ed4 and its exact raw source links in the manifest. Port its rotated stage order consistently; do not mix that order with the paper Algorithm 1 placement of divergence correction. Declare source-cohort insertion and birth-age treatment as a separate adaptation before simulation. Keep reference pressure signs, matched fluid-pair terms and safeguards. The reference uses .8d^3 per particle; this pilot explicitly uses d^3 for cubic packing and source accounting. Verify its initial cubic-kernel density normalization in the compact control; do not silently copy .8d^3 or tune normalization after a failed run. Akinci 2012 stationary boundary samples with quadrature volumes supply all matching density, factor, rate and pressure terms; boundary samples are not water mass. Deduplicate coincident boundary samples. Record contact radius, friction/restitution and first-hit remainder handling before running.

Integrate source discharge analytically into a reservoir, with stable particle IDs and exactly-once admission and outlet removal. Place births across the actual opening without rounding its area or changing Q/A. Record actual birth times and per-particle age over each step; never apply a full pre-birth gravity step. Declare how new cohorts enter pressure stages. Do not solve the entire world separately for every birth quantum. Source off affects future admission only. Sorting must preserve every particle field and ID. Clear newborn pressure history.

The pre-run manifest must fix solver stage order; kernel and mass normalization; Jacobi relaxation; density/divergence iteration caps and stopping units; warm-start scaling or explicitly disabled initial warm-start; pressure clamp; dimensioned factor regularization; source spatial/age rule; swept collision policy; reference differences; and actual device limits. GPU Jacobi reads the previous iterate. Dispatch boundaries separate global dependencies. Count only final applied wall impulses, never the sum of trial-iteration forces.

Exact pinned-source inspection corrected the preliminary reading: fewer than 20 total neighbors masks the initial divergence pressure and reported convergence residual, but the subsequent Jacobi update uses the unmasked source residual. Density pressure is unaffected. Port that exact behavior initially and disclose both raw and masked convergence; do not describe it as total pressure exclusion. Report the affected count, mass share and locations plus raw and clamped density/rate. A mostly deficient falling stream or a sheet supplied only by surface smoothing cannot pass the thin-water gate. Do not invent a replacement pressure formula to conceal this limitation.

### Independent measurements

These are new representation-specific error limits, declared before results. They do not retroactively pass any old geometric candidate or lower the final realism goal.

| Measurement | Candidate gate and required disclosure |
| --- | --- |
| Source/material/reservoir/outlet accounting | Maximum closure error 1e-9 m3; count-based closure alone is not incompressibility. |
| Independent physical volume | At most 1% error using fixed calibrated world-space occupancy/surface and basin sections; not sum(m/rho) or a per-frame fitted isovalue. |
| Direct material momentum | Each-axis tolerance max(1e-6 kg m/s, 1e-5 times admitted mass in kg times Uref=8 m/s). Also report the original 1e-6 threshold. Only measured gravity, boundary and outlet/source exchange count as external impulses. |
| Bulk compression | Mean positive density error below .1%, p99 below 1%; disclose maximum and full-population distribution, with surface deficiency separate. |
| Density-rate residual | Report raw dot-rho/rho0 in 1/s and dt-scaled positive residual; mean below .1%, p99 below 1% for the solved bulk. An iteration-cap miss fails. |
| Hydrostatic control | RMS parasitic speed below .005 m/s and level drift below .002 m over ten seconds; refinement must improve. |
| Ownership and walls | No duplicate source/outlet ownership or solid tunnelling. Kernel overlap is intentional; solid penetration and premature cross-gap interaction are measured separately and must shrink under refinement. |
| Surface evidence | Fixed world-space reconstruction, independent volume check, raw support alongside shaded water, front/side/orbit/underwater inspection. Target mean surface-motion error .00625 m where an independent reference exists. No globally shifted/thickened geometry to pass. |

Only three compact prerequisites precede the cascade: source/on/off timing, hydrostatic wall behavior, and two-stream/solid-impact momentum. Compare selected captured GPU states with a Float64 reference using the same equations. Then run the actual cascade; do not expand this into another static certification project. At physical times 1, 4, 6, 8 and 10 s, retain source identity, states, compression/deficiency/volume/momentum, event times, outlet exchange, and stage costs. Reuse W05 fixed-time event and outlet refinement limits. Physical energy losses and numerical dissipation stay separately visible.

### GPU capacity, runtime and bounded stop

Keep particle state, neighbor search, pressure, contact and world-space reconstruction GPU resident. CPU handles UI/control and bounded summaries. Use complete sorted neighbor ranges, not silently truncated neighbor arrays. No dense allocation of the entire fine surface domain. Maximum particle slots are 131072 at 20 mm and 1048576 at 10 mm. Declare boundary, bin, surface-sample and vertex capacities and total peak bytes before dispatch; compare each allocation with the actual adapter and binding limits. Overflow, device loss and incomplete convergence freeze the candidate with diagnostics. Allocation limits are not evidence the capacity is sufficient.

Measure the complete water workload per 16.7 ms of physical time, including every substep, neighbor build, pressure iteration, source/contact stage, reconstruction, transfer and water draw. Candidate budget remains 8 ms p95 and at most 4.8 wall seconds of water work per ten physical seconds. Record CPU/GPU timing validity, adapter, startup, mean/p95 and physical-time lag. An 8 ms substep does not meet an 8 ms frame budget when many substeps are required. The first debug run may be explicitly slow; it cannot pass runtime acceptance.

Each physical attempt is capped at ten minutes wall time; preserve the last valid state at the cap and report incomplete progress. A failed physical invariant, overflow or representation failure freezes the run immediately. Across this candidate permit two evidence-directed repairs/optimizations, then renew Pro architecture research before a third. Keep old and new hashes distinct. The first advancing valid state should become inspectable immediately with its limits, not replayed as a finished waterfall.

Workflow improvement: choose an established pressure method and spend the next cycle on an advancing complete cascade plus independent physical, visual and cost evidence. Keep manifest/preflight short and front-load the actual thin-water and GPU risks. Internal material samples may support the physics; visible beads, fixed curtains, baked animation, compensating foam and degraded native optics remain unacceptable.
